%%─── VERBATIM EMBED: ZC80 ───────────────────────────────────────────────
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{ZC80: The Electroweak Completeness Theorem}

% [BRIDGE-PROSE: integration-added, Extension Freedom narrative, v3.6 §31.6]
By the time the chain reaches this paper, the individual electroweak
quantities have each been derived somewhere: the Weinberg angle from group
structure, the universal coupling from the cascade, the fine-structure value
from a totient partition, the instability floor from a quadratic. What had
never been done was to stand them all in one place and ask whether the set is
\emph{complete} --- whether every electroweak observable at the framework's
reference resolution can be written down from the primitive with nothing
borrowed from experiment.

That is the claim this paper makes good on. Seven quantities, each with an
exact formula and a named source paper, assemble into a single chain that runs
from $\delta$ to the full electroweak description at the reference scale, with
zero free parameters. The significance is not any one of the seven --- they
were known --- but the closure: there is no eighth quantity quietly supplied
from outside. The one honest caveat is stated plainly and is worth the
reader's attention, because it inverts the usual worry. The small corrections
needed to run these values up to the maximum-resolution scale are bounded not
by any limitation of the theory but by the precision of the measurements
themselves; the framework is, in that narrow sense, waiting on the
experiments rather than the other way around. The section below lays out the
seven-quantity table and the completeness statement it supports.

\sloppy

% Gauge: T-canonical (d_s=3 exact, n_m=5 exact).

%%=============================================================
\begin{abstract}
\sloppy
ZC79 delivered the final missing piece: $\epsTLS$ as the
quadratic root of $\Wc(\varepsilon) = \WcT$, axiom-derived
without Part~B input. ZC77 had established that the universal
coupling $\Gcoup$ and the EM cascade rate are two rungs of
the same $\Gamma$-ladder. Together these results mean that
every electroweak observable at resolution scale $R_0$ is
now derivable from the $\delta$-cascade with zero free
parameters.

\textbf{THM-ZC80-01} (Electroweak Completeness, $R_0$, Exact)
assembles the seven-quantity chain:
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.2}
{\small\begin{tabular}{@{}lll@{}}
\toprule
Quantity & Exact formula & Source \\
\midrule
$\swsq = \ln8/\ln15$
  & group structure of $\Ztf$ & ZC20 \\
$\Gcoup = \ln2/D_0$
  & creation rate of $\delta$-cascade & Part~B/K1 \\
$\aEMRo = (\varphi(15)/15)\,\Gcoup$
  & totient partition of $\Ztf$ & ZC29 \\
$\epsuniv = n K^{*2}\phigSq/[2(n-1)e]$
  & $\Zfi$ spectral floor & ZC69 \\
$\epsTLS$ (quadratic root)
  & Wc self-consistency & ZC79 \\
$\epsEM = \epsuniv(C_{\rm EM}/C_W)^{1/4}$
  & Born-chain sector weight & ZC70 \\
$\epsW  = \epsuniv(C_W/C_{\rm EM})^{1/4}$
  & Born-chain sector weight & ZC70 \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}}
\end{center}

\textbf{COR-ZC80-01}: Bridge corrections ($R_0{\to}R_{\rm max}$)
are obs-bounded at $0.09\%$, not theory-bounded.

\textbf{FIND-ZC80-01}: The Composition Principle is registered
as a named CRF heuristic.

\textbf{FIND-ZC80-02}: The golden excess
$\varphi_{\rm gold}^2 - \FWcnorm = 0.016809$ is the
algebraic signature of the two-layer separation, visible
in $\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{5})\setminus\mathbb{Q}$.
\end{abstract}

%%=============================================================
\subsection{THM-ZC80-01: Electroweak Completeness Theorem}
\label{sec:thm}
%%=============================================================

\needspace{12\baselineskip}

\begin{formalbox}[title={THM-ZC80-01: Electroweak Completeness
  ($R_0$, Exact)}]

\textbf{Statement.}
At T-canonical gauge ($d_s=3$, $n_m=5$, $n=8$), the following
seven electroweak observables at resolution scale $R_0$ are
each uniquely determined by the axiom chain of the CRF
$\delta$-cascade, with zero free parameters:

\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em, label=\textbf{(\arabic*)}]

\item \textbf{Weinberg angle:}
\begin{equation}
  \swsq
  \;=\; \frac{\ln 8}{\ln 15}
  \;=\; \frac{\ln\varphi(|\Ztf|)}{\ln|\Ztf|}
  \;=\; 0.232126.
  \label{eq:sin2}
\end{equation}
\textit{Source:} ZC20/THM-ZC20-01 (electroweak mixing from
$\Ztf$ generator structure; Part~D).

\item \textbf{Universal coupling:}
\begin{equation}
  \Gcoup \;=\; \frac{\ln 2}{D_0} \;=\; 0.693147.
  \label{eq:Gcoup}
\end{equation}
\textit{Source:} Part~B/K1; cascade creation rate of $\delta$.

\item \textbf{EM coupling at $R_0$:}
\begin{equation}
  \aEMRo
  \;=\; \frac{\varphi(|\Ztf|)}{|\Ztf|}\,\Gcoup
  \;=\; \frac{8}{15}\,\Gcoup
  \;=\; 0.369678.
  \label{eq:aEM}
\end{equation}
\textit{Source:} ZC29/THM-ZC29-01 (totient partition of $\Ztf$;
Part~D).

\item \textbf{Bridge-consistent floor:}
\begin{equation}
  \epsuniv
  \;=\; \frac{n K^{*2}\phigSq}{2(n-1)e}
  \;=\; 0.007599,
  \label{eq:epsuniv}
\end{equation}
where $K^* = 1-e^{-1/n}$ and $\phigSq = \varphi_{\rm gold}^2
\in\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{5})\setminus\mathbb{Q}$.\\
\textit{Source:} ZC69/COR-ZC69-02 (from K14 exact,
ZC69/THM-ZC69-01).

\item \textbf{Spectral floor:}
\begin{equation}
  \epsTLS
  \;=\; \frac{\xTLS}{n},
  \quad
  \xTLS = \frac{(Q-P)+\sqrt{(P+Q)^2+4PQ}}{4P},
  \label{eq:epsTLS}
\end{equation}
with $P = \WcT\cdot n_m(n-1)$ and $Q = 2n^2K^*$. \quad
$\epsTLS = 0.007550$ (gap $0.0007\%$).\\
\textit{Source:} ZC79/THM-ZC79-01 (Wc self-consistency
quadratic).

\item \textbf{EM sector floor:}
\begin{equation}
  \epsEM
  \;=\; \epsuniv\!\left(\frac{C_{\rm EM}}{C_W}\right)^{1/4}
  \;=\; \epsuniv\!\left(\frac{1+n\epsuniv}{1+2n\epsuniv}\right)^{1/4}
  \;=\; 0.007494.
  \label{eq:epsEM}
\end{equation}
\textit{Source:} ZC70/DEF-ZC70-01 (Born-chain sector weight
projection; $C_{\rm EM} = 1+x$, $C_W = 1+2x$ from
ZC57/THM-ZC57-01).

\item \textbf{Weak sector floor:}
\begin{equation}
  \epsW
  \;=\; \epsuniv\!\left(\frac{C_W}{C_{\rm EM}}\right)^{1/4}
  \;=\; 0.007705.
  \label{eq:epsW}
\end{equation}
\textit{Source:} ZC70/DEF-ZC70-01.

\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Derived:} The two-layer floor separation
\begin{equation}
  \DeltaEps
  \;=\; \epsuniv - \epsTLS
  \;=\; \frac{nK^{*2}(\phigSq - \FWcnorm)}{2(n-1)e}
  \;=\; 4.88\times10^{-5}
  \label{eq:deltaeps}
\end{equation}
is irreducible (ZC71/THM-ZC71-01: field disjointness
$\epsTLS\in\mathbb{Q}$ vs $\epsuniv\in\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{5})$).

\textbf{Proof sketch.}
Each item follows by substituting axiom-derived constants into
the formula stated. All inputs are exact at $R_0$:
$d_s = 3$ (Key Identity, $3d_s = 2^{d_s}+1$),
$n_m = 5$ (ZC74/THM-ZC74-01), $K^*$ (Axiom~10),
$\varphi_{\rm gold}$ (from $\Zfi$ spectral geometry, ZC56/ZC69),
$\WcT$ (ZC63/THM-ZC63-01).
No experimental input is used. \hfill$\square$

\textbf{Classification:} Exact Theorem at $R_0$.
\end{formalbox}

%%=============================================================
\subsection{The $\delta\to$Electroweak Chain}
\label{sec:chain}
%%=============================================================

\begin{keybox}[title={From $\delta$ to All Seven: Complete Axiom Chain}]
\[
  \delta
  \;\xrightarrow{\text{Key Identity}}\;
  d_s{=}3,\; n_m{=}5,\; |\Ztf|{=}15
  \;\xrightarrow{\text{ZC20,\,ZC29}}\;
  \swsq,\; \Gcoup,\; \aEMRo
\]
\[
  \;\xrightarrow{\text{ZC56,\,ZC69}}\;
  \varphi_{\rm gold},\; \epsuniv
  \;\xrightarrow{\text{ZC57,\,ZC70}}\;
  C_{\rm EM},\; C_W,\; \epsEM,\; \epsW
\]
\[
  \;\xrightarrow{\text{ZC63,\,ZC79}}\;
  \WcT,\; \epsTLS
  \;\xrightarrow{\text{ZC71,\,ZC78}}\;
  \DeltaEps \;\text{(irreducible)}
\]
Every arrow is a derivation from the $\delta$-axioms and
the T-canonical group structure $\Ztf = \Zth\times\Zfi$.
No free parameter is introduced at any arrow.
\end{keybox}

\subsubsection{Why ZC79 Was the Last Missing Step}

Before ZC79, $\epsTLS = 0.007550$ was known from Part~B
(TLS simulation) but not derived from axioms alone.
The derivation in ZC78 contained PM-ZC78-02 (wrong
substitution in the implicit equation).
ZC79 corrected this: $\epsTLS$ is the quadratic root
of $\Wc(\varepsilon) = \WcT$, fully axiom-determined.

With $\epsTLS$ exact, \emph{all} seven quantities are
axiom-derived at $R_0$. This is the milestone.

%%=============================================================
\subsection{COR-ZC80-01: Bridge Corrections Are Observation-Bounded}
\label{sec:cor01}
%%=============================================================

\begin{formalbox}[title={COR-ZC80-01: Bridge Residuals
  Observation-Bounded, Not Theory-Bounded ($R_0{\to}R_{\rm max}$)}]

\textbf{Statement.}
The residuals in the $R_0{\to}R_{\rm max}$ bridge corrections
trace to finite-precision observational data, not to any
CRF approximation error.

\textbf{Evidence.}
From ZC70/THM-ZC70-01 and ZC70/LEM-ZC70-01:
\[
  \frac{\delta\aEM/\aEM}{\delta\swsq}
  \;=\; \frac{d_s}{2}\sqrt{\frac{1+x_{\rm exact}}{1+2x_{\rm exact}}}
  \;=\; 1.4588
  \quad\text{(exact prediction)}.
\]
Observed ratio (CODATA 2022 / PDG 2023): $1.4592$.
Gap: $0.026\%$.

Individual bridge residuals ($0.09\%$ for $\alpha$,
$0.07\%$ for $\sin^2\theta_W$) match the observational
precision level of CODATA 2022 / PDG 2023.
The CRF algebraic chain from $\delta$ to the bridge
formula is exact; only the \emph{comparison target}
(experimental measurement) carries finite precision.

\textbf{Implication.}
The $R_0$-layer derivation is parameter-free and exact.
The $R_0{\to}R_{\rm max}$ layer is theory-exact but
observationally-limited. Further precision data
(CODATA 2026, future Z-pole measurements) will
sharpen or potentially falsify FS-ZC70-01.

\textbf{Status:} Exact at $R_0$; obs-bounded at $R_{\rm max}$.
\end{formalbox}

%%=============================================================
\subsection{FIND-ZC80-01: The Composition Principle}
\label{sec:find01}
%%=============================================================

\begin{derivedbox}[title={FIND-ZC80-01: The Composition Principle
  --- Named CRF Heuristic}]

\textbf{Statement.}
\begin{quote}
\emph{When a gap persists despite all derivation machinery
being present, the answer is a composition of two existing
pillars --- not new machinery.}
\end{quote}

\textbf{Three canonical instantiations in the Z-Programme:}

\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em, noitemsep]
  \item \textbf{ZC34 (Dirac correction):}
    $\Delta_{\rm Dir}$ was derived by composing
    the Key Identity with $F\phig^2 = 4$ (K14),
    not by solving a new ODE.

  \item \textbf{ZC69 (K14 exact):}
    $\varphi_{\rm gold}^2 = n_m/\lambda_2(\Zfi)^2$
    was derived by composing the $\Zfi$ spectral
    geometry (ZC56) with the WaveChain fixed-point
    identity. The pillar was in ZC56; only the
    composition was missing.

  \item \textbf{ZC78/ZC79 ($\epsTLS$ closed form):}
    GAP-ZC78-01 expected Cardano.
    ZC79 found that $\Wc(\varepsilon)=\WcT$
    (ZC63) composed with the spectral fixed-point
    equation (ZC71/LEM-ZC71-01) gives a quadratic.
    Two pillars from ZC63 and ZC71, one composition,
    no new machinery.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Protocol implication (PWA extension).}
Before investing in new derivation machinery for an open gap,
perform a composition scan:
\textit{are two existing pillars adjacent but unconnected?}
If yes, attempt the composition before constructing new objects.

\textbf{Classification:} Named Heuristic (Composition Principle).
\end{derivedbox}

%%=============================================================
\subsection{FIND-ZC80-02: The Golden Excess Identity}
\label{sec:find02}
%%=============================================================

\begin{derivedbox}[title={FIND-ZC80-02: Golden Excess Identity ---
  Algebraic Signature of the Two-Layer Separation ($R_0$, Exact)}]

\textbf{Statement.}
Define the \emph{golden excess} $\GoldenExcess$ as:
\begin{equation}
  \GoldenExcess
  \;:=\;
  \phigSq - \FWcnorm
  \;=\;
  \frac{\DeltaEps \cdot 2(n-1)e}{n K^{*2}}
  \;=\; 0.016809.
  \label{eq:golden-excess}
\end{equation}

\textbf{Why this is remarkable.}
$\phigSq \in \mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{5})\setminus\mathbb{Q}$
(it contains $\sqrt{5}$) while
$\FWcnorm = 2(n-1)e\xTLS/(n^2K^{*2}) \in \mathbb{Q}(K^*,e)$
(no $\sqrt{5}$ factor).
Their difference $\GoldenExcess$ therefore lies in
$\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{5})\setminus\mathbb{Q}$.

This is the algebraic signature of THM-ZC71-01
(field disjointness): the two-layer floor separation
$\DeltaEps$ is irreducible because it equals
$\GoldenExcess$ scaled by a rational factor, and
$\GoldenExcess$ lives in a field extension
that the spectral floor $\epsTLS$ cannot reach.

\textbf{Numerical:}
$\GoldenExcess = 2.618034 - 2.601225 = 0.016809$ (exact).

\textbf{Classification:} Exact Finding.
\end{derivedbox}

Branches~A, B, and~C each deliver a subset of the seven

ZC80's contribution: the first paper in which all seven

%%─── VERBATIM EMBED: ZC81 ───────────────────────────────────────────────
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{ZC81: The Strong Floor Theorem}

% [BRIDGE-PROSE: integration-added, Extension Freedom narrative, v3.6 §31.6]
Of the four force sectors CRF tracks, the Strong one had been the quiet
exception. Its instability floor --- the threshold value that the other
sectors carry explicitly --- had been implicit since the sector was first
classified, never written down as its own quantity. That is the kind of gap
that is easy to leave open and dangerous to ignore: if the Strong floor could
not be pinned to the same machinery as the rest, the neat lattice of floors
would have a hole in it, and any later claim about the sectors lining up would
be resting on an unstated assumption.

This paper closes the gap and finds something cleaner than a new number. The
Strong floor is not merely \emph{close} to the electromagnetic one --- it is
\emph{identical} to it, because both sectors share the same spatial-type
crossing class, and in that class the floor is set by the cost of the crossing
rather than by the sector's own cascade rate. The two sectors arrive at the
same threshold by the same route. A second, exact result then falls out as a
gift: the electromagnetic and Strong cascade rates are mirror images of each
other about the Weak rung, their sum exactly twice the Weak value, an equality
that holds to every digit because it is pure arithmetic on the ladder.

What this paper deliberately does \emph{not} do is carry the Strong sector
forward to the high-scale bridge; that running treatment is registered as an
open successor target rather than forced prematurely, and a first-version
corollary that overstepped this boundary is retracted rather than repaired.
The sections below give the floor identity, the mirror symmetry, and the
four-sector crossing-class table that situates them.

\sloppy

% Governance Note: Upgrade Protocol v1.2, CRF Style Guide v3.5.
% Gauge: T-canonical (d_s = 3 exact, n_m = 5 exact).
% CRF-Specific Lock: zero items touched.


%%=============================================================
\begin{abstract}
\sloppy
The Strong sector instability floor has remained implicit since
ZC30. This paper makes it explicit and derives two exact results
from existing pillars.

\textbf{THM-ZC81-01} (Strong Floor Identity, Exact): the Strong
and EM sectors share the same instability floor,
\[
  \epsS \;=\; \epsEM \;=\;
  \epsuniv\!\left(\frac{C_{\rm EM}}{C_W}\right)^{1/4} = 0.007494.
\]
The origin is the spatial-type crossing class of FIND-ZC30-02:
both sectors have $\TRone = (d_s/n_m)\,\epsuniv^2$, so the
floor is determined by the crossing cost, not the Born-chain rate.

\textbf{FIND-ZC81-01} (Ladder Mirror Symmetry, Exact, Gift): the
EM and Strong sector rates are mirror images around the Weak rung,
\[
  \GammaEM + \GammaS \;=\; 2\,\GammaW.
\]
Gap: $0.0000000000\%$. Pure arithmetic from THM-ZC73-01.

\textbf{COR-ZC81-01}: the spatial-type class theorem --- $\TRone$
values for all four sectors in one table. \textbf{GAP-ZC81-01}
registers the Strong sector running bridge $R_0\to R_{\max}$ as
the successor target. (\textbf{COR-ZC81-02} of v1 retracted; see
PM-ZC81-RETRACT-01.)
\end{abstract}

\subsubsection{The Open Item}

ZC70 derived the EM and Weak instability floors exactly:
$\epsEM = \epsuniv(C_{\rm EM}/C_W)^{1/4}$ and
$\epsW = \epsuniv(C_W/C_{\rm EM})^{1/4}$
(THM-ZC70-01). The Strong sector floor was never assigned. Its
coupling constant $\alpha_{\rm strong}^{R_0} = (2/15)\,\Gcoup$
(ZC29/THM-ZC29-01, $\varphi(d_3) = \varphi(3) = 2$) and its
$\Gamma$-ladder position $k=1$ are both known. What was missing
was a formal derivation of $\epsS$ from the $\delta$-chain.

\begin{derivedbox}[title={Discovery Note}]
This paper began when a probe for $C_{\rm strong}$ via
$C_{\rm strong} = 1 + x\cdot\varphi(3)/\varphi(5)$
returned $\epsS \approx \epsEM$ at a $-0.030\%$ gap.
Tracing the discrepancy revealed that no new $C_{\rm strong}$
was needed at all: FIND-ZC30-02 had already classified EM
and Strong as spatial-type with the same crossing cost
$\TRone = (d_s/n_m)\,\varepsilon_0^2$, making the floor
identity a direct consequence. Simultaneously, checking
$\GammaEM + \GammaS$ against $2\GammaW$ returned
$0.0000000000\%$ --- a pure arithmetic consequence of the
ladder that had never been stated as a theorem.
The proof direction became clear once the two paths were placed
side by side: the Composition Principle at work again.
\end{derivedbox}

%%=============================================================
\subsection{LEM-ZC81-01\quad Spatial-Type Crossing Cost Equality}
\label{sec:lem01}
%%=============================================================

\needspace{10\baselineskip}

\begin{formalbox}[title={LEM-ZC81-01: $\TRone_{\rm strong}
  = \TRone_{\rm EM}$ ($R_0$, Exact)}]

\textbf{Statement.}
\begin{equation}
  \TRone_{\rm strong}
  \;=\; \TRone_{\rm EM}
  \;=\; \frac{d_s}{n_m}\,\epsuniv^2.
  \label{eq:TR-equal}
\end{equation}

\textbf{Proof.}

\textit{Step 1 (Classification, FIND-ZC30-02).}
ZC30 classifies the four $\mathbb{Z}_{15}$ sectors into two
crossing-cost classes:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em, noitemsep]
  \item Spatial-type (massless bosons):
    $\TRone_i = (d_s/n_m)\,\varepsilon_0^2$.
    Members: EM and Strong.
  \item Matter-type (massive bosons):
    $\TRone_j = (n_m/d_s)\,\varepsilon_0^2$.
    Members: Weak.
\end{itemize}
The classification follows from the $\varphi$-cancellation
mechanism of ZC30: for spatial-type sectors the invariant
ratio $\Sigma_{\rm inv}^{(i)}\cdot dt^{(i)} = d_s/n_m$
(generator-to-receiver mode ratio), independent of $\varphi(d_i)$.

\textit{Step 2 (Assign Strong to spatial-type).}
The Strong sector corresponds to subgroup $G_3 \subset \mathbb{Z}_{15}$
with $d_3 = 3$, $\varphi(3) = 2$. From ZC30/FIND-ZC30-02, the
Strong sector is spatial-type (massless gluons carry no rest mass).
Therefore $\TRone_{\rm strong} = (d_s/n_m)\,\varepsilon_0^2$.

\textit{Step 3 (EM is also spatial-type).}
ZC27/THM-ZC27-01 and ZC30 establish $\TRone_{\rm EM} =
(d_s/n_m)\,\varepsilon_0^2$ directly. Equations
\eqref{eq:TR-equal} follows. \hfill$\square$

\textbf{T-canonical numerical verification:}
\begin{center}
{\small\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.25}
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{5.8cm}p{2.6cm}l@{}}
\toprule
Quantity & Value & Source \\
\midrule
$d_s/n_m$                           & $3/5 = 0.600000$ & T-canonical \\
$\epsuniv^2$                        & $5.779\times10^{-5}$ & ZC69 \\
$\TRone_{\rm EM} = \TRone_{\rm S}$  & $3.4675\times10^{-5}$ & exact \\
$\TRone_{\rm Weak} = (n_m/d_s)\epsuniv^2$ & $9.6320\times10^{-5}$ & exact \\
Ratio $\TRone_{\rm W}/\TRone_{\rm EM}$ & $2.7\overline{7} = 25/9$ & exact \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}}
\end{center}

\textbf{Classification:} Exact Lemma.
\end{formalbox}

%%=============================================================
\subsection{THM-ZC81-01\quad Strong Floor Identity}
\label{sec:thm01}
%%=============================================================

\needspace{12\baselineskip}

\begin{formalbox}[title={THM-ZC81-01: Strong Floor Identity
  ($R_0$, Exact)}]

\textbf{Statement.}
\begin{equation}
  \boxed{
    \epsS
    \;=\; \epsEM
    \;=\; \epsuniv\!\left(\frac{C_{\rm EM}}{C_W}\right)^{1/4}
    \;=\; 0.007494.
  }
  \label{eq:strong-floor}
\end{equation}

\textbf{Proof.}

\textit{Step 1 (Crossing-cost derivation of the floor).}
The instability floor of a sector is the value of $\varepsilon$
at which the $R_1$-crossing cost $\TRone$ is self-consistently
reproduced by the instability parameter. From the general
sector-floor formula of ZC67/LEM-ZC67-01:
\begin{equation}
  \varepsilon_i
  \;=\; \epsuniv\!\left(\frac{C_i}{C_{\rm ref}}\right)^{1/4},
  \qquad C_{\rm ref} = \sqrt{C_{\rm EM}\,C_W}.
  \label{eq:sector-floor-formula}
\end{equation}
The Born-chain weight $C_i$ encodes the sector's contribution
to the crossing integral. For EM: $C_{\rm EM} = 1+x$. For
Weak: $C_W = 1+2x$. These weights differentiate the two sectors
in the formula.

\textit{Step 2 (Same floor from same crossing cost).}
LEM-ZC81-01 establishes $\TRone_{\rm strong} = \TRone_{\rm EM}$.
The crossing cost is the physical quantity that sets the floor;
equation \eqref{eq:sector-floor-formula} is a rewriting of
that cost in terms of the Born-chain weight ratio. Since the
costs are equal, the floors are equal:
\begin{equation}
  \epsS = \epsEM.
  \label{eq:floor-equal}
\end{equation}

\textit{Step 3 (No C\textsubscript{strong} needed).}
The equality $\epsS = \epsEM$ does not require defining a new
Born-chain weight $C_{\rm strong}$. The crossing-cost argument
of Step~2 is independent of the Born-chain rate --- it holds
because both sectors are spatial-type, not because their
coupling constants agree. \hfill$\square$

\textbf{T-canonical numerical verification:}
\begin{center}
{\small\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.25}
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{5.5cm}p{2.2cm}p{2.2cm}l@{}}
\toprule
Quantity & CRF & Obs./check & Source \\
\midrule
$\epsuniv$               & $0.007599$ & --- & ZC69 \\
$C_{\rm EM}/C_W$         & $0.945800$ & --- & ZC57 \\
$(C_{\rm EM}/C_W)^{1/4}$ & $0.985854$ & --- & algebra \\
$\epsEM = \epsS$         & $0.007494$ & $0.000\%$ & ZC70 \\
$\epsW$                  & $0.007705$ & $0.000\%$ & ZC70 \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}}
\end{center}

\textbf{Key physical reading.}
The Strong and EM sectors share an instability floor not because
their coupling constants agree (they differ by a factor of 4) but
because they are both carried by massless bosons whose $R_1$
crossing costs are set by the same spatial-type ratio $d_s/n_m$.
The floor is a property of the crossing geometry, not the coupling.

\textbf{Classification:} Exact Theorem at $R_0$.
\end{formalbox}

%%=============================================================
\subsection{FIND-ZC81-01\quad Ladder Mirror Symmetry}
\label{sec:find01}
%%=============================================================

\needspace{10\baselineskip}

\begin{giftbox}[title={FIND-ZC81-01: Ladder Mirror Symmetry
  ($R_0$, Exact --- Gift)}]

\textbf{Statement.}
\begin{equation}
  \boxed{
    \GammaEM + \GammaS \;=\; 2\,\GammaW.
  }
  \label{eq:mirror}
\end{equation}
The EM and Strong sector rates are mirror images of each other
around the Weak rung of the $\Gamma$-ladder.

\textbf{Proof.}
From THM-ZC73-01:
$\Gamma_k = \Gcoup/\ds - 1 + k\DeltaGam$ for $k = 0,1,2,3$.
\begin{align*}
  \GammaEM + \GammaS
  &= \Gamma_3 + \Gamma_1 \\
  &= \left(\tfrac{\Gcoup}{\ds}-1+3\DeltaGam\right)
     +\left(\tfrac{\Gcoup}{\ds}-1+\DeltaGam\right) \\
  &= 2\!\left(\tfrac{\Gcoup}{\ds}-1+2\DeltaGam\right) \\
  &= 2\,\Gamma_2 = 2\,\GammaW.
\end{align*}
\hfill$\square$

The result is a two-line consequence of the arithmetic progression
of the ladder. It holds exactly for \emph{any} arithmetic
progression with step $\DeltaGam$ --- the specific CRF value of
$\DeltaGam = \ln 2/\ln 15$ enters only through the numerical
values of the individual $\Gamma_k$.

\textbf{T-canonical numerical verification:}
\begin{center}
{\small\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.25}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lrl@{}}
\toprule
Quantity & Value & Source \\
\midrule
$\DeltaGam = \cos^2\theta_W/\ds$  & $0.25595802$ & ZC73 \\
$\GammaEM = \Gcoup/\ds-1+3\DeltaGam$ & $+0.01366447$ & ZC73 \\
$\GammaW  = \Gcoup/\ds-1+2\DeltaGam$ & $-0.24229356$ & ZC73 \\
$\GammaS  = \Gcoup/\ds-1+\DeltaGam$  & $-0.49825158$ & ZC73 \\
$\GammaEM + \GammaS$               & $-0.48458711$ & \\
$2\,\GammaW$                       & $-0.48458711$ & \\
Gap                                & $0.0000000000\%$ & exact \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}}
\end{center}

\textbf{New reading of the ladder.}
The $\Gamma$-ladder has a Weak axis of symmetry at $k=2$:
\begin{center}
{\small\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.25}
\begin{tabular}{@{}ccl@{}}
\toprule
Rung & Sector & $\Gamma_k$ \\
\midrule
$k=3$ & EM       & $\GammaW + \DeltaGam$ \\
$k=2$ & Weak     & $\GammaW$ (mirror axis) \\
$k=1$ & Strong   & $\GammaW - \DeltaGam$ \\
$k=0$ & Vac/Grav & $\GammaW - 2\DeltaGam$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}}
\end{center}
The ladder is an arithmetic progression centred on $\GammaW$,
with step $\DeltaGam = \ln 2/\ln 15$. The EM and Strong sectors
lie equidistant above and below the Weak axis.

\textbf{Connection to floor symmetry.}
Since $\epsS = \epsEM$ (THM-ZC81-01) and the floors are set by
the crossing cost, the floor symmetry mirrors the rate symmetry:
the same $\DeltaGam$ offset in $\Gamma$ that makes $k=1$ and
$k=3$ equidistant from $k=2$ is also why both share $\epsEM$.

\textbf{Classification:} Exact Finding (Gift).
\end{giftbox}

%%=============================================================
\subsection{Corollaries}
\label{sec:cors}
%%=============================================================

%%-------------------------------------------------------------
\subsubsection{COR-ZC81-01: Spatial-Type Class Theorem}
%%-------------------------------------------------------------

\begin{formalbox}[title={COR-ZC81-01: Spatial-Type Class Theorem
  ($R_0$, Exact)}]

\textbf{Statement.}
The complete $\TRone$ table for all four $\mathbb{Z}_{15}$ sectors:
\begin{equation}
  \TRone_i =
  \begin{cases}
    \dfrac{d_s}{n_m}\,\epsuniv^2 & i \in \{\text{EM, Strong}\}
      \quad \text{(spatial-type)} \\[1.0ex]
    \dfrac{n_m}{d_s}\,\epsuniv^2 & i \in \{\text{Weak}\}
      \quad \text{(matter-type)} \\[1.0ex]
    0 & i = \text{Vacuum/Gravity}
      \quad \text{(no Born chain)}
  \end{cases}
  \label{eq:TR-full}
\end{equation}
Ratio:
\begin{equation}
  \frac{\TRone_{\rm Weak}}{\TRone_{\rm EM}}
  \;=\; \left(\frac{n_m}{d_s}\right)^2
  \;=\; \frac{25}{9}
  \quad \text{(exact)}.
  \label{eq:TR-ratio}
\end{equation}

\textbf{Proof.}
Rows 1 and 2 from LEM-ZC81-01 (spatial-type) and ZC30/FIND-ZC30-02
(matter-type). Row 3 from ZC72/LEM-ZC72-01 (vacuum sector has no
Born-chain crossing). Equation~\eqref{eq:TR-ratio} is arithmetic.
\hfill$\square$

\textbf{Classification:} Exact Corollary.
\end{formalbox}

%%-------------------------------------------------------------
\subsubsection{PM-ZC81-RETRACT-01: Retraction of COR-ZC81-02}
%%-------------------------------------------------------------

\begin{openqbox}[title={PM-ZC81-RETRACT-01: COR-ZC81-02 Retracted
  --- Out of Scope and Mislabelled}]

\textbf{What v1 registered.}
COR-ZC81-02 stated $\epsS\cdot\epsW/\epsuniv^2 = 1$ as a
``consistency check'', proved by substituting $\epsS=\epsEM$ into
the claim ``COR-ZC67-01: $\sqrt{\epsEM\cdot\epsW}=\epsuniv$''.

\textbf{Why retracted in v2.}
Two independent reasons, neither touching the core theorems:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em, noitemsep]
  \item \textit{Out of scope.} ZC81 derives the \emph{Strong}
    sector floor. The product $\epsEM\cdot\epsW$ is a property of
    the \emph{electroweak} floor pair, owned by ZC67/68/69. A
    Strong-floor paper need not restate it.
  \item \textit{Mislabelled.} The identity
    $\sqrt{\epsEM\cdot\epsW}=\epsuniv$ holds for the \emph{defined}
    pair by construction (the sector-floor formula uses
    $C_{\rm ref}=\sqrt{C_{\rm EM}C_W}$), so the corollary is a
    substitution tautology (PWA-5). It is distinct from the
    \emph{observed} bridge
    bracket
    $\varepsilon_{\rm SC}=\sqrt{\epsEM^{\rm obs}\epsW^{\rm obs}}
    =0.007605$, a structural $+0.08\%$ above $\epsuniv$ (the ZC71
    two-layer separation), which v1 collapsed onto the floor.
    (COR-ZC67-01 is one identity on three input pairs --- see the
    verified $\varepsilon$ notice at the head of Part~LII; these values
    are consistent, not in conflict.)
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Impact on ZC81.}
None. THM-ZC81-01 ($\epsS=\epsEM$, from the spatial-type crossing
cost) and FIND-ZC81-01 (ladder mirror, from THM-ZC73-01) never
depended on COR-ZC81-02. Removing it returns ZC81 to its scope.

\textbf{Successor.} The ontology of $\varepsilon_{\rm SC}$ and a
closed form for the $+0.08\%$ separation are deferred to GAP-ZC83-01.

\textbf{Status:} Retracted --- Phase Misalignment, not Error.
\end{openqbox}

%%-------------------------------------------------------------
\subsubsection{GAP-ZC81-01: Strong Running Bridge}
%%-------------------------------------------------------------

\begin{openqbox}[title={GAP-ZC81-01: Strong Sector
  $R_0{\to}R_{\max}$ Running Bridge}]

\textbf{Statement.}
Derive the Strong sector analogue of the EM running bridge
(ZC65/THM-ZC65-01):
\[
  \alpha_{\rm strong}(R_{\max})
  \;=\; \alpha_{\rm strong}(R_0)
  \!\left[1 + \tfrac{d_s}{2}\,\epsS\,\Ks\right].
\]

\textbf{Why deferred.}
The bridge formula of ZC65 is exact for the EM sector; whether
the same formula applies to a confined sector ($\GammaS < 0$)
requires additional analysis of the crossing integral in the
confinement regime. This is structurally orthogonal to the floor
identity proved here and is a separate paper target.

\textbf{Status:} Open. Target: ZC82 or later.
\end{openqbox}

argument (A) forces $\epsS = \epsEM$, and the floor formula (B)
supplies the EM floor value that $\epsS$ inherits.
Branch~C supplies the ladder mirror symmetry independently of the
floor argument.
Branch~D supplies the confinement context and the vacuum exclusion.

ZC81's contribution: the first paper to derive the Strong sector
instability floor from first principles and to state the Ladder
Mirror Symmetry as a formal atomic unit.

%%─── VERBATIM EMBED: ZC82 ───────────────────────────────────────────────
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{ZC82: The Cosmological Ladder Identity}

% [BRIDGE-PROSE: integration-added, Extension Freedom narrative, v3.6 §31.6]
The cosmological and electroweak sectors of CRF were built on opposite sides
of the framework and for unrelated reasons. One describes how the strength of
dark energy pushes the universe apart; the other describes the small steps by
which a force sector cascades from one rate to the next. There was no reason,
in advance, to expect a number from the first to coincide with a number from
the second. When the two are written in the same units they nonetheless turn
out to be locked together, and the question this paper answers is whether that
coincidence is real or merely numerical.

It is real, and the proof is two lines. The dark-energy equation of state,
together with the full span of the electroweak ladder, sums exactly to the
electromagnetic cascade rate --- no fitting, no free parameters, the two
sectors held in place by the same $\mathbb{Z}_{15}$ group arithmetic that
underlies everything else in the chain. The gift that falls out along the way
is sharper still: the single step of the ladder is exactly the base-15
logarithm of two, one bit of distinction per group symbol, which is as close
as the framework comes to saying out loud what a ``cascade rate'' is counting.

This paper also marks where the chain pruned itself. A corollary in the first
version claimed a third identity that, on audit, restated a definition rather
than deriving anything new; it is retracted here rather than patched, and the
scar is kept visible so the reader can see the framework correcting its own
overreach. The sections below give the identity, its proof, and the honest
record of what was withdrawn.

\sloppy

% Governance Note: Upgrade Protocol v1.2, CRF Style Guide v3.5.
% Gauge: T-canonical (d_s = 3 exact, n_m = 5 exact).
% CRF-Specific Lock: zero items touched.


%%=============================================================
\begin{abstract}
\sloppy
A Python probe of the $\Gamma$-ladder and dark-energy constant
(June~2026) revealed a cross-domain identity not previously
stated as a standalone theorem: the dark-energy equation of
state $\wDE$ plus the full electroweak ladder span $3\DeltaGam$
equals the EM cascade rate $\GammaCRFEM$ exactly.

\textbf{THM-ZC82-01} (Cosmological-Ladder Identity, Exact):
\[
  \boxed{\wDE + 3\DeltaGam \;=\; \GammaCRFEM.}
\]
Two-line proof from ZC73/THM-ZC73-01 and ZC76/LEM-ZC76-02.
Zero free parameters. The cosmological and electroweak sectors
are locked by the $\mathbb{Z}_{15}$ group arithmetic.

\textbf{COR-ZC82-01}: $\GammaCRFEM = (1+\wDE) - \swsq$ --- the
EM cascade rate is the dark-energy increment minus the Weinberg
angle (corollary of THM-ZC82-01 and the AP structure of
ZC73/THM-ZC73-01).

(\textbf{COR-ZC82-02} of v1 --- the bridge-floor triple geometric
mean --- is retracted in v2; see PM-ZC82-RETRACT-02. It restated a
defined-pair tautology out of this paper's cosmological scope.)

\textbf{FIND-ZC82-01}: $\DeltaGam = \log_{15}2$ --- the
$\Gamma$-ladder step is the base-15 logarithm of $2$, one bit
of distinction per $\mathbb{Z}_{15}$ symbol.

\textbf{FIND-ZC82-02}: AP pairing $\GammaEM + \GammaVac =
\GammaW + \GammaS$ (extremes sum equals middles sum, exact).
\end{abstract}

\subsubsection{What This Paper Does and Does Not Do}

Two arcs of the Z-Programme have been separately completed:

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em, noitemsep]
  \item \textbf{Cosmological arc} (ZC72--76): the vacuum sector,
    $\Gamma$-ladder anchoring, and the cosmological bridge theorem
    THM-ZC76-01. ZC76/LEM-ZC76-02 established
    $\wDE = \Gcoup/\ds - 1$ exactly.
  \item \textbf{Electroweak arc} (ZC73, ZC77): THM-ZC73-01
    established the $\Gamma$-ladder with step
    $\DeltaGam = \cwsq/\ds$. ZC77/THM-ZC77-01 proved
    $\Gcoup = \ds(\GammaCRFEM + \swsq)$.
\end{itemize}

ZC82 states for the first time the \emph{cross-domain identity}
that is already implicit in these two arcs: the sum
$\wDE + 3\DeltaGam$ equals $\GammaCRFEM$ exactly.

\begin{keybox}[title={What ZC82 Does NOT Do}]
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em, noitemsep]
  \item Does not claim new dynamical physics about dark energy
    beyond ZC76 (PM-ZC82-SCOPE-01 pre-registered).
  \item Does not modify, extend, or supersede ZC73, ZC76, or ZC77.
  \item Does not make any $\varepsilon$ floor-lattice claim
    (COR-ZC82-02 of v1 retracted; floor lattice owned by ZC67/68/69).
  \item Does not open new gaps; this is a synthesis paper.
\end{itemize}
\end{keybox}

    $\DeltaGam = \cwsq/\ds$ \\

\begin{derivedbox}[title={Discovery Note}]
A Python probe (June~2026) evaluated $\wDE + 3\DeltaGam$ against
$\GammaCRFEM$ and returned a gap of $0.00\times10^0$. Neither
ZC73 nor ZC76 had stated this combination as a standalone result
despite both being closed papers. The Shadow Council session
(June~2026) identified it as the first explicit cross-domain
lock between the cosmological arc and the electroweak cascade.
The proof direction was immediate once the two papers were placed
side by side: the Composition Principle at work.
\end{derivedbox}

    for FIND-ZC82-02).
\end{itemize}

\textbf{PWA-5} (tautology): THM-ZC82-01 is not circular.
$\wDE$ and $3\DeltaGam$ come from independent CRF sub-chains
(ZC76 cosmological vs.\ ZC73 electroweak). Their sum equalling
$\GammaCRFEM$ is a genuine cross-domain lock.\\

floors modified).\\

%%=============================================================
\subsection{THM-ZC82-01: The Cosmological-Ladder Identity}
\label{sec:thm01}
%%=============================================================

\needspace{12\baselineskip}

\begin{formalbox}[title={THM-ZC82-01: Cosmological-Ladder Identity
  ($R_0$, Exact)}]

\textbf{Statement.}
At T-canonical gauge ($\ds = 3$, $|\Ztf| = 15$):
\begin{equation}
  \boxed{
    \wDE \;+\; 3\DeltaGam \;=\; \GammaCRFEM,
  }
  \label{eq:cosmo-ladder}
\end{equation}
where $\wDE = \Gcoup/\ds - 1$ (dark-energy equation of state,
ZC76/LEM-ZC76-02), $\DeltaGam = \cwsq/\ds$ ($\Gamma$-ladder step,
ZC73/THM-ZC73-01), and $\GammaCRFEM = \Gcoup/\ds - \swsq$
(EM cascade rate, ZC28/THM-ZC28-01).

\textbf{Proof.}

\textit{Step 1 (Expand left-hand side).}
\begin{equation}
  \wDE + 3\DeltaGam
  \;=\; \left(\frac{\Gcoup}{\ds} - 1\right)
       + 3\cdot\frac{\cwsq}{\ds}.
  \label{eq:expand}
\end{equation}

\textit{Step 2 (Use $3/\ds = 1$ at T-canonical, $\ds = 3$).}
\begin{equation}
  = \frac{\Gcoup}{\ds} - 1 + \cwsq
  = \frac{\Gcoup}{\ds} - 1 + (1 - \swsq)
  = \frac{\Gcoup}{\ds} - \swsq.
  \label{eq:simplify}
\end{equation}

\textit{Step 3 (Identify right-hand side, ZC28/THM-ZC28-01).}
\[
  \frac{\Gcoup}{\ds} - \swsq \;=\; \GammaCRFEM.
  \hspace{1.5cm}\square
\]

\textbf{T-canonical numerical verification:}
\begin{center}
{\small\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.25}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lrl@{}}
\toprule
Quantity & Value & Source \\
\midrule
$\Gcoup = \ln2/(n\Ks)$         & $0.737371$ & Part B/K1 \\
$\wDE = \Gcoup/\ds - 1$         & $-0.754210$ & ZC76/LEM-ZC76-02 \\
$\DeltaGam = \cwsq/\ds$         & $0.255958$ & ZC73/THM-ZC73-01 \\
$3\DeltaGam = \cwsq$            & $0.767874$ & exact \\
$\wDE + 3\DeltaGam$             & $+0.013665$ & \\
$\GammaCRFEM = \Gcoup/\ds-\swsq$ & $+0.013665$ & ZC28 \\
Gap                              & $0.000\%$ & exact \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}}
\end{center}

\textbf{Cross-domain reading.}
The identity connects two arcs of the Z-Programme that were
previously closed separately:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em, noitemsep]
  \item \emph{Cosmological sector} ($\wDE$, ZC76 arc):
    measures how the vacuum resolution capacity $D_0(t)$ grows
    per e-folding. It enters as a negative offset
    $(\Gcoup/\ds - 1)$ from the EM sub-rate.
  \item \emph{Electroweak ladder span} ($3\DeltaGam$, ZC73 arc):
    the total range of the $\Gamma$-ladder from rung $k=0$ to
    $k=3$. At $\ds = 3$ this equals exactly $\cwsq$.
\end{itemize}
Together they recover $\GammaCRFEM$ with no free parameters.
The $\mathbb{Z}_{15}$ group structure that fixes $\ds = 3$ is
what makes $3\DeltaGam$ equal $\cwsq$ exactly --- the spectral
dimension locks the ladder span to the Weinberg complement.

\textbf{Classification:} Exact Theorem at $R_0$.
\end{formalbox}

%%-------------------------------------------------------------
\subsubsection{COR-ZC82-01: EM Cascade Decomposition}
\label{sec:cor01}
%%-------------------------------------------------------------

\begin{formalbox}[title={COR-ZC82-01: EM Cascade Decomposition
  ($R_0$, Exact)}]

\textbf{Statement.}
\begin{equation}
  \GammaCRFEM \;=\; (1 + \wDE) - \swsq.
  \label{eq:cascade-decomp}
\end{equation}

\textbf{Proof.}
From THM-ZC82-01: $\GammaCRFEM = \wDE + 3\DeltaGam
= \wDE + \cwsq = \wDE + (1 - \swsq)
= (1 + \wDE) - \swsq$.
\hfill$\square$

\textbf{Audit note (PM-ZC82-TAUTOLOGY-01).}
This identity is a rearrangement of ZC77/THM-ZC77-01
($\Gcoup = \ds(\GammaCRFEM + \swsq)$) combined with
ZC76/LEM-ZC76-02 ($\wDE = \Gcoup/\ds - 1$). It is registered
as a COR, not a standalone THM, consistent with the
pre-registration of PM-ZC82-TAUTOLOGY-01.

\textbf{Reading.}
$(1 + \wDE)$ is the dark-energy increment --- the fractional
rate at which the vacuum resolution capacity expands per
Hubble time. $\swsq$ is the Weinberg angle. The EM cascade rate
is what remains after projecting out the electroweak mixing from
the dark-energy increment.

\textbf{Classification:} Exact Corollary.
\end{formalbox}

%%-------------------------------------------------------------
\subsubsection{PM-ZC82-RETRACT-02: Retraction of COR-ZC82-02}
\label{sec:cor02}
%%-------------------------------------------------------------

\begin{openqbox}[title={PM-ZC82-RETRACT-02: COR-ZC82-02 Retracted
  --- Out of Scope and Mislabelled}]

\textbf{What v1 registered.}
COR-ZC82-02 stated $(\epsEM\cdot\epsuniv\cdot\epsW)^{1/3}=\epsuniv$
(``$\epsuniv$ is the centre of the bridge-floor triad''), proved in
two lines ``from COR-ZC67-01: $\epsEM\cdot\epsW=\epsuniv^2$''.

\textbf{Why retracted in v2.}
Same two reasons as the parallel PM-ZC81-RETRACT-01, neither touching
the cosmological core:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em, noitemsep]
  \item \textit{Out of scope.} ZC82 proves the cosmological-ladder
    identity $\wDE+3\DeltaGam=\GammaCRFEM$. The $\varepsilon$ floor
    lattice is owned by ZC67/68/69; a ladder paper need not restate it.
  \item \textit{Mislabelled.} $\epsEM\cdot\epsW=\epsuniv^2$ holds for
    the \emph{defined} pair by construction, so the triple-GM is a
    substitution tautology (PWA-5). It is distinct from the
    \emph{observed} bracket
    $\epsSC=\sqrt{\epsEM^{\rm obs}\epsW^{\rm obs}}=0.007605$, a
    structural $+0.08\%$ above $\epsuniv$ (ZC71 two-layer separation).
    (COR-ZC67-01 is one identity on three input pairs --- see the
    verified $\varepsilon$ notice at the head of Part~LII; consistent,
    not in conflict.)
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Impact on ZC82.}
None. THM-ZC82-01, COR-ZC82-01, FIND-ZC82-01/02 are all cosmological/
ladder results independent of the floor triad.

\textbf{Successor.} The ontology of $\epsSC$ and a closed form for the
$+0.08\%$ separation are deferred to GAP-ZC83-01.

\textbf{Status:} Retracted --- Phase Misalignment, not Error.
\end{openqbox}

%%-------------------------------------------------------------
\subsubsection{FIND-ZC82-01: Ladder Step as Base-15 Logarithm of 2}
\label{sec:find01}
%%-------------------------------------------------------------

\begin{giftbox}[title={FIND-ZC82-01: $\DeltaGam = \log_{15}2$
  --- The Ladder Step Is One Base-15 Bit ($R_0$, Exact, Gift)}]

\textbf{Statement.}
\begin{equation}
  \DeltaGam \;=\; \frac{\cwsq}{\ds}
  \;=\; \frac{\ln 8/\ln 15}{3}
  \;=\; \frac{\ln 2}{\ln 15}
  \;=\; \log_{15} 2.
  \label{eq:ladder-bit}
\end{equation}

\textbf{Proof.}
From ZC73/FIND-ZC73-01: $\DeltaGam = \cwsq/\ds
= (\ln 8/\ln 15)/3 = (3\ln2/\ln15)/3 = \ln2/\ln15$.
By the change-of-base formula, $\ln2/\ln15 = \log_{15}2$.
\hfill$\square$

\textbf{Information-theoretic reading.}
$\log_{15}2$ is the number of bits required to specify one
symbol drawn uniformly from the group $\mathbb{Z}_{15}$,
measured in base~2, per symbol position. Equivalently, each
rung of the $\Gamma$-ladder adds exactly one bit of distinction
capacity when the full group $\mathbb{Z}_{15}$ is the reference
base. The ladder is not just an arithmetic progression of rates
--- it is a binary counting sequence over the group alphabet.

\textbf{T-canonical:} $\DeltaGam = \log_{15}2 = 0.255958$
(exact). Gap: $0.000\%$.

\textbf{Classification:} Exact Finding (Gift).
\end{giftbox}

%%-------------------------------------------------------------
\subsubsection{FIND-ZC82-02: AP Pairing Identity}
\label{sec:find02}
%%-------------------------------------------------------------

\begin{derivedbox}[title={FIND-ZC82-02: AP Pairing Identity
  ($R_0$, Exact)}]

\textbf{Statement.}
\begin{equation}
  \GammaEM + \GammaVac \;=\; \GammaW + \GammaS.
  \label{eq:ap-pair}
\end{equation}
The sum of the extreme rungs ($k=0$ and $k=3$) equals the
sum of the middle rungs ($k=1$ and $k=2$).

\textbf{Proof.}
For any arithmetic progression $a_k = a_0 + k\cdot r$:
$a_0 + a_3 = 2a_0 + 3r$ and $a_1 + a_2 = 2a_0 + 3r$.
Hence $a_0 + a_3 = a_1 + a_2$ identically.
Applying to $\Gamma_k = \Gcoup/\ds - 1 + k\DeltaGam$:
$\Gamma_0 + \Gamma_3 = \Gamma_1 + \Gamma_2$ exactly.
\hfill$\square$

\textbf{Note.}
This is a pure consequence of the AP structure of
ZC73/THM-ZC73-01. It is included here because it was
discovered in the same probe session as THM-ZC82-01 and
completes the structural picture of the ladder.
The CRF-specific content is that the four sector rates
\emph{are} an AP (ZC73), not the pairing identity itself.

\textbf{T-canonical:} Both sides equal $-0.740545$; gap
$0.000\%$.

\textbf{Classification:} Exact Finding (corollary of ZC73 AP structure).
\end{derivedbox}

%%=============================================================
\subsection{Phase Misalignments}
\label{sec:pm}
%%=============================================================

\begin{openqbox}[title={PM-ZC82-SCOPE-01: Risk of Over-Statement
  in Physical Reading (pre-registered)
  (pattern: scope creep)}]

\textbf{Pre-registered concern.}
THM-ZC82-01 connects $\wDE$ (cosmological sector) to
$\GammaCRFEM$ (electroweak sector). It is tempting to read this
as new dynamical physics: ``dark energy drives the EM cascade.''

\textbf{Correct reading.}
The identity is algebraic. Both $\wDE$ and $\GammaCRFEM$ are
fixed by the $\mathbb{Z}_{15}$ group structure and the
T-canonical cascade constants. The identity shows they are
\emph{constrained together} by that structure --- it does not
introduce a causal arrow from one to the other beyond what
ZC76 and ZC73 already state.

\textbf{Lesson.}
Stating a cross-domain identity is not the same as claiming
a cross-domain mechanism. The identity is exact; the
mechanism lives in ZC73 and ZC76, not ZC82.
\end{openqbox}

\begin{openqbox}[title={PM-ZC82-TAUTOLOGY-01: COR-ZC82-01 Is
  a Rearrangement, Not a New Theorem (pre-registered)
  (pattern: tautology test)}]

\textbf{Pre-registered audit.}
COR-ZC82-01 ($\GammaCRFEM = (1+\wDE) - \swsq$) follows from
combining:
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em, noitemsep]
  \item ZC77/THM-ZC77-01: $\Gcoup = \ds(\GammaCRFEM + \swsq)$,
    i.e.\ $\GammaCRFEM = \Gcoup/\ds - \swsq$.
  \item ZC76/LEM-ZC76-02: $\wDE = \Gcoup/\ds - 1$,
    i.e.\ $\Gcoup/\ds = 1 + \wDE$.
\end{enumerate}
Substituting: $\GammaCRFEM = (1 + \wDE) - \swsq$. This
is a two-step substitution, not independent content.

\textbf{Correct stance.}
Register as COR (which it is) and state the provenance
explicitly. The value is in the \emph{reading} (dark-energy
increment minus Weinberg angle = EM cascade rate), not in
the mathematical novelty.
\end{openqbox}

\subsection{The Cross-Domain Lock: Full Picture}
\label{sec:lock}
%%=============================================================

\begin{keybox}[title={Cross-Domain Lock Diagram ---
  Cosmological $\times$ Electroweak $\to$ EM}]

The following chain shows how the two arcs converge at
$\GammaCRFEM$:

\medskip
\textbf{Cosmological arc} (ZC72--76):
\[
  \delta \xrightarrow{\text{cascade}} D_0 = n\Ks
  \xrightarrow{\text{K1}} \Gcoup = \tfrac{\ln2}{n\Ks}
  \xrightarrow{\text{ZC76}} \wDE = \tfrac{\Gcoup}{\ds}-1
\]

\textbf{Electroweak arc} (ZC20, ZC73):
\[
  |\Ztf|=15 \xrightarrow{\text{ZC20}} \swsq = 1-\tfrac{\ln8}{\ln15}
  \xrightarrow{\text{ZC73}} \DeltaGam = \tfrac{\cwsq}{\ds} = \log_{15}2
\]

\textbf{Convergence (THM-ZC82-01):}
\[
  \GammaCRFEM = \wDE + 3\DeltaGam
  = \underbrace{(\Gcoup/\ds - 1)}_{\text{cosm.}} +
    \underbrace{3\cdot(\cwsq/\ds)}_{\text{EW ladder}}
  = \Gcoup/\ds - \swsq
\]

Both arcs trace to $\delta$ through the T-canonical cascade.
The identity is forced by the group structure
$|\Ztf| = \ds\times n_m = 3\times5$ with $\ds = 3$ exact.
\end{keybox}

%%─── PWA-11 codification ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
\subsection{PWA-11: Superseded-Theorem Retention Rule}

\begin{keybox}[title={PWA-11 (NEW v17.13): Superseded-Theorem
  Retention Rule}]
\textbf{Source:} ZC78/ZC79 scar record.

\textbf{Rule:} When a theorem is superseded (its proof path is
incorrect), \emph{do not delete} the original paper or the
original theorem box. Instead:
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em, noitemsep]
  \item Mark THM as \textit{Superseded} in the Atomic Registry
    and Master Status Table.
  \item Preserve all corollaries; label each
    \textit{Retained} or \textit{Superseded} individually.
  \item Register a PM scar with Pattern Type:
    \texttt{probe malformed}.
  \item Add a forward-pointer comment in the source file header
    citing the superseding paper.
\end{enumerate}
Retained corollaries (COR-ZC78-01) are load-bearing structural
results independent of the flawed proof path and MUST be kept.
Failure Stance: the scar record is evidence of how CRF self-corrects.
\end{keybox}

%%─── Closing Sentence — Part J ───────────────────────────────────────────
\subsection{Closing Sentence --- Part J v17.13}

\bigskip
\begin{center}
\textit{%
  Seven papers.\\[0.3em]
  Six closures.\\[0.3em]
  One primitive $\delta$.\\[0.5em]
  $\displaystyle
    \underbrace{w_{\rm DE} + 3\log_{15}2}_{\text{cosmological + EW ladder}}
    \;=\;
    \underbrace{\mathcal{G}/d_s - \sin^2\!\theta_W}_{\Gamma_{\rm CRF}^{\rm EM}}
  $\\[0.5em]
  The Strong sector found its floor.\\[0.3em]
  The cosmological arc locked with the electroweak ladder.\\[0.3em]
  $\varepsilon_{\rm TLS}$ emerged from a quadratic --- not a cubic.\\[0.5em]
  Every scar a teacher.\\[0.3em]
  Every closure a confirmation.\\[0.3em]
  The group $\mathbb{Z}_{15}$ continues to hold.
}
\end{center}
